People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I guess… people at work started noticing I’m doing weird stuff that doesn’t really make sense. Like, I work with receipts and numbers, and I’ll end up lining receipts across the floor instead of filing them. Or I keep tapping the calculator in sets of nine, which sounds stupid when I say it out loud.

And the copier thing too—I’ve been standing there with my hands up, just kind of stuck there, until someone asks if I’m okay. The copier at my school used to jam all the time too, actually, and I always thought it had this one specific sound before it messed up, but… yeah, I guess that’s not the point. I’m here because I’m doing things like that and people are getting concerned.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… sometimes. I mean, with the invoices and stuff, it can feel like the totals or account numbers aren’t just random. Like they’re… pointing at me somehow, or like the due dates line up in a way that’s meant for me to notice.

I know that sounds weird. My supervisor will show me it’s just a normal entry, like, “See, this is just from this vendor,” and I’ll understand what he’s saying, but I still kind of feel like they’re tracking whether I get the pattern or not. It’s hard to explain.

Numbers have always stuck in my head, though. Like I used to remember everyone’s locker combinations in school, not because I was trying to be creepy, just because numbers kind of stay there. But with the accounts it feels different, like there’s a message in it.

Sometimes, yeah. Not all the time. It’s usually when I’m by myself, like at my desk, or walking home from class, which is funny because I don’t even take classes now, but I still think of it that way because the route goes past the school buildings.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not exactly like… “if I don’t do this, something terrible happens.” It’s more that I just start doing it and it feels like it has to be finished a certain way.

Like at work I’ll line receipts across the floor, which I know doesn’t make sense because then people have to step around them, and that’s not even where receipts go. Or I’ll tap the calculator in sets of nine. Nine is a weird number because it looks like a six upside down, which used to bother me on worksheets in school if the page got turned around—

But, yeah, I don’t think I’m preventing something bad. It’s just… I’m doing these things, like standing by the copier with my hands held up, and then someone asks if I’m okay and I realize how odd it must look.

They come and go, yeah. It’s not every minute or anything. But it’s been… building, I guess, for about ten months now. Around when I started the evening accounting courses while still doing the bookkeeping job.

It’s not like a whole conversation, usually just a phrase.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you must perform these actions, like lining receipts or tapping the calculator, in order to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like… “if I don’t do this, something terrible happens.” It’s more that I just start doing it and it feels like it has to be finished a certain way.\n\nLike at work I’ll line receipts across the floor, which I know doesn’t make sense because then people have to step around them, and that’s not even where receipts go. Or I’ll tap the calculator in sets of nine. Nine is a weird number because it looks like a six upside down, which used to bother me on worksheets in school if the page got turned around—\n\nBut, yeah, I don’t think I’m preventing something bad. It’s just… I’m doing these things, like standing by the copier with my hands held up, and then someone asks if I’m okay and I realize how odd it must look.\n\nDoctor: Do these things, like lining up receipts or hearing the voice, happen all the time or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: They come and go, yeah. It’s not every minute or anything. But it’s been… building, I guess, for about ten months now. Around when I started the evening accounting courses while still doing the bookkeeping job.\n\nAt first I thought it was just being tired, because those classes were after work and the fluorescent lights in those rooms are awful—like they buzz in a way that makes numbers feel sharper, if that makes sense. But over time it’s been happening more in patches. Some days I’m mostly okay, and other days it’s like the receipts or the voice or the patterns are harder to ignore.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you believe the voice you hear is actually trying to communicate something meaningful to you?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 22 / bookkeeping_accounting_or_auditing_clerk / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I know it sounds strange, but I get convinced that certain invoice totals, account numbers, and due dates are messages about me, and even when my supervisor shows me they are normal entries, I still feel like they are tracking whether I understand the pattern.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I mean, I know it sounds strange, but sometimes it feels like it’s not just random. Like when it says something and then I look at an invoice total or an account number, or even a due date, I get this feeling that it’s kind of… pointing at something about me.

My supervisor will show me, like, “No, that’s just a normal entry, that’s just how the account is labeled,” and I can understand that when he says it. But part of me still feels like the numbers are tracking whether I understand the pattern or not. Like I’m supposed to notice it.

It’s hard to explain without sounding ridiculous. The dates especially can feel like they line up too neatly, and then I start thinking maybe the voice is trying to get me to see that.

Sometimes it feels like that, yeah… not exactly “line up the receipts now,” but I’ll hear a man’s voice when I’m alone at my desk, or walking home from class, and it’ll say things like, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign.”

Which is embarrassing, because once I did that near the bus stop and this lady thought I was looking at her bag or something—anyway. Like I’ll be at work with the receipts and then they’re across the floor in these lines, and I didn’t exactly plan that. Or I’m tapping the calculator in nines and then I realize someone is watching me.\n\nThe copier thing is weird too—I’ll just be standing there with my hands up, like I’m waiting for something, and then someone has to ask if I’m okay. I don’t know if I “have to” do it. It just sort of happens, and then it looks strange.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel distressed or anxious when you realize you're doing these things, like lining up receipts or standing by the copier?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it anxious exactly. Sometimes I feel embarrassed when I notice somebody saw it, like when you realize your shirt’s been inside out all morning or something. That happened to me once in high school, actually, and nobody told me until lunch, which was kind of… yeah.\n\nWith the receipts or the copier, it’s more like I come back to myself and think, “Oh, I’m doing that again.” I don’t always know what I’m feeling right then. Maybe uncomfortable, but I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that there's a special meaning or connection between things that might not be obvious to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I mean, with the invoices and stuff, it can feel like the totals or account numbers aren’t just random. Like they’re… pointing at me somehow, or like the due dates line up in a way that’s meant for me to notice.\n\nI know that sounds weird. My supervisor will show me it’s just a normal entry, like, “See, this is just from this vendor,” and I’ll understand what he’s saying, but I still kind of feel like they’re tracking whether I get the pattern or not. It’s hard to explain.\n\nNumbers have always stuck in my head, though. Like I used to remember everyone’s locker combinations in school, not because I was trying to be creepy, just because numbers kind of stay there. But with the accounts it feels different, like there’s a message in it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear voices or sounds that other people can't hear?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yeah. Not all the time. It’s usually when I’m by myself, like at my desk, or walking home from class, which is funny because I don’t even take classes now, but I still think of it that way because the route goes past the school buildings.\n\nIt’s like a man’s voice, pretty clear. It’ll say something like, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign,” and I’ll actually look around because it sounds like someone said it near me. But then there’s nobody there. It’s not like a whole conversation, usually just a phrase.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you must perform these actions, like lining receipts or tapping the calculator, in order to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like… “if I don’t do this, something terrible happens.” It’s more that I just start doing it and it feels like it has to be finished a certain way.\n\nLike at work I’ll line receipts across the floor, which I know doesn’t make sense because then people have to step around them, and that’s not even where receipts go. Or I’ll tap the calculator in sets of nine. Nine is a weird number because it looks like a six upside down, which used to bother me on worksheets in school if the page got turned around—\n\nBut, yeah, I don’t think I’m preventing something bad. It’s just… I’m doing these things, like standing by the copier with my hands held up, and then someone asks if I’m okay and I realize how odd it must look.\n\nDoctor: Do these things, like lining up receipts or hearing the voice, happen all the time or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: They come and go, yeah. It’s not every minute or anything. But it’s been… building, I guess, for about ten months now. Around when I started the evening accounting courses while still doing the bookkeeping job.\n\nAt first I thought it was just being tired, because those classes were after work and the fluorescent lights in those rooms are awful—like they buzz in a way that makes numbers feel sharper, if that makes sense. But over time it’s been happening more in patches. Some days I’m mostly okay, and other days it’s like the receipts or the voice or the patterns are harder to ignore.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice you hear is actually trying to communicate something meaningful to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I know it sounds strange, but sometimes it feels like it’s not just random. Like when it says something and then I look at an invoice total or an account number, or even a due date, I get this feeling that it’s kind of… pointing at something about me.\n\nMy supervisor will show me, like, “No, that’s just a normal entry, that’s just how the account is labeled,” and I can understand that when he says it. But part of me still feels like the numbers are tracking whether I understand the pattern or not. Like I’m supposed to notice it.\n\nIt’s hard to explain without sounding ridiculous. The dates especially can feel like they line up too neatly, and then I start thinking maybe the voice is trying to get me to see that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like the voice you hear is telling you to do these things, like lining up receipts or tapping the calculator?\n\nPatient: Sometimes it feels like that, yeah… not exactly “line up the receipts now,” but I’ll hear a man’s voice when I’m alone at my desk, or walking home from class, and it’ll say things like, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign.”\n\nAnd it’s clear enough that I actually look around, like someone was behind me. Which is embarrassing, because once I did that near the bus stop and this lady thought I was looking at her bag or something—anyway. It’s not always telling me to do the tapping exactly, but when it says something like that, I start checking things or looking for what it means.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. The dates especially can feel like they line up too neatly, and then I start thinking maybe the voice is trying to get me to see that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like the voice you hear is telling you to do these things, like lining up receipts or tapping the calculator?\n\nPatient: Sometimes it feels like that, yeah… not exactly “line up the receipts now,” but I’ll hear a man’s voice when I’m alone at my desk, or walking home from class, and it’ll say things like, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign.”\n\nAnd it’s clear enough that I actually look around, like someone was behind me. Which is embarrassing, because once I did that near the bus stop and this lady thought I was looking at her bag or something—anyway. It’s not always telling me to do the tapping exactly, but when it says something like that, I start checking things or looking for what it means.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you feel like the voice is directly telling you to perform these specific actions, like lining up the receipts or tapping the calculator?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. Not like a command exactly… I don’t think it says, “tap the calculator nine times,” like that. But then I’ll find myself doing it, you know? Like tapping in sets of nine, or lining receipts across the floor at work, and afterward I’m kind of standing there thinking, why did I do that?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t always feel like I’m choosing it in a normal way. I’ll be at work and suddenly the receipts are spread out across the floor, like I’m lining them up for some reason, and then I’m aware of people looking at me.

Or I’ll tap the calculator in sets of nine, and it’s not like that’s part of bookkeeping, obviously. My teacher in high school used to make us show our work on calculators, which is funny because now I’m doing the opposite, like it’s not helping at all—sorry, that’s not really the point.

The point is, yeah, when I’m doing those things, it feels like something is pushing me toward it. Like standing by the copier with my hands up until someone asks if I’m okay… I don’t really know why I did that. It just felt connected somehow.

Like I’ll be at work with the receipts and then they’re across the floor in these lines, and I didn’t exactly plan that. Or I’m tapping the calculator in nines and then I realize someone is watching me.\n\nThe copier thing is weird too—I’ll just be standing there with my hands up, like I’m waiting for something, and then someone has to ask if I’m okay. I don’t know if I “have to” do it. It just sort of happens, and then it looks strange.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel distressed or anxious when you realize you're doing these things, like lining up receipts or standing by the copier?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it anxious exactly. Sometimes I feel embarrassed when I notice somebody saw it, like when you realize your shirt’s been inside out all morning or something. That happened to me once in high school, actually, and nobody told me until lunch, which was kind of… yeah.\n\nWith the receipts or the copier, it’s more like I come back to myself and think, “Oh, I’m doing that again.” I don’t always know what I’m feeling right then. Maybe uncomfortable, but I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that there's a special meaning or connection between things that might not be obvious to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I mean, with the invoices and stuff, it can feel like the totals or account numbers aren’t just random. Like they’re… pointing at me somehow, or like the due dates line up in a way that’s meant for me to notice.\n\nI know that sounds weird. My supervisor will show me it’s just a normal entry, like, “See, this is just from this vendor,” and I’ll understand what he’s saying, but I still kind of feel like they’re tracking whether I get the pattern or not. Sort of, but not like a list of instructions. It’s more like… when I’m alone at my desk, or sometimes walking home from class, I hear this man’s voice say things like, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign.”

It just sort of happens, and then it looks strange.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel distressed or anxious when you realize you're doing these things, like lining up receipts or standing by the copier?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it anxious exactly. Sometimes I feel embarrassed when I notice somebody saw it, like when you realize your shirt’s been inside out all morning or something. That happened to me once in high school, actually, and nobody told me until lunch, which was kind of… yeah.\n\nWith the receipts or the copier, it’s more like I come back to myself and think, “Oh, I’m doing that again.” I don’t always know what I’m feeling right then. Maybe uncomfortable, but I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that there's a special meaning or connection between things that might not be obvious to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I mean, with the invoices and stuff, it can feel like the totals or account numbers aren’t just random. Like they’re… pointing at me somehow, or like the due dates line up in a way that’s meant for me to notice.\n\nI know that sounds weird. My supervisor will show me it’s just a normal entry, like, “See, this is just from this vendor,” and I’ll understand what he’s saying, but I still kind of feel like they’re tracking whether I get the pattern or not. It’s hard to explain.\n\nNumbers have always stuck in my head, though. Like I used to remember everyone’s locker combinations in school, not because I was trying to be creepy, just because numbers kind of stay there. But with the accounts it feels different, like there’s a message in it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear voices or sounds that other people can't hear?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yeah. Not all the time. It’s usually when I’m by myself, like at my desk, or walking home from class, which is funny because I don’t even take classes now, but I still think of it that way because the route goes past the school buildings.\n\nIt’s like a man’s voice, pretty clear. It’ll say something like, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign,” and I’ll actually look around because it sounds like someone said it near me. But then there’s nobody there. It’s not like a whole conversation, usually just a phrase.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you must perform these actions, like lining receipts or tapping the calculator, in order to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like… “if I don’t do this, something terrible happens.” It’s more that I just start doing it and it feels like it has to be finished a certain way.\n\nLike at work I’ll line receipts across the floor, which I know doesn’t make sense because then people have to step around them, and that’s not even where receipts go. Or I’ll tap the calculator in sets of nine. Nine is a weird number because it looks like a six upside down, which used to bother me on worksheets in school if the page got turned around—\n\nBut, yeah, I don’t think I’m preventing something bad. It’s just… I’m doing these things, like standing by the copier with my hands held up, and then someone asks if I’m okay and I realize how odd it must look.\n\nDoctor: Do these things, like lining up receipts or hearing the voice, happen all the time or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: They come and go, yeah. It’s not every minute or anything. But it’s been… building, I guess, for about ten months now. Around when I started the evening accounting courses while still doing the bookkeeping job.\n\nAt first I thought it was just being tired, because those classes were after work and the fluorescent lights in those rooms are awful—like they buzz in a way that makes numbers feel sharper, if that makes sense. But over time it’s been happening more in patches. Some days I’m mostly okay, and other days it’s like the receipts or the voice or the patterns are harder to ignore.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice you hear is actually trying to communicate something meaningful to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I know it sounds strange, but sometimes it feels like it’s not just random. Like when it says something and then I look at an invoice total or an account number, or even a due date, I get this feeling that it’s kind of… pointing at something about me.\n\nMy supervisor will show me, like, “No, that’s just a normal entry, that’s just how the account is labeled,” and I can understand that when he says it. But part of me still feels like the numbers are tracking whether I understand the pattern or not. Like I’m supposed to notice it.\n\nIt’s hard to explain without sounding ridiculous. The dates especially can feel like they line up too neatly, and then I start thinking maybe the voice is trying to get me to see that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like the voice you hear is telling you to do these things, like lining up receipts or tapping the calculator?\n\nPatient: Sometimes it feels like that, yeah… not exactly “line up the receipts now,” but I’ll hear a man’s voice when I’m alone at my desk, or walking home from class, and it’ll say things like, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign.”\n\nAnd it’s clear enough that I actually look around, like someone was behind me. Which is embarrassing, because once I did that near the bus stop and this lady thought I was looking at her bag or something—anyway. It’s not always telling me to do the tapping exactly, but when it says something like that, I start checking things or looking for what it means.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice is directly telling you to perform these specific actions, like lining up the receipts or tapping the calculator?\n\nPatient: Not like a command exactly… I don’t think it says, “tap the calculator nine times,” like that. But then I’ll find myself doing it, you know? Like tapping in sets of nine, or lining receipts across the floor at work, and afterward I’m kind of standing there thinking, why did I do that?\n\nAnd the copier thing too—I stood there with my hands up by the copier until someone asked if I was okay. I don’t even know why. It’s like the voice or the feeling points me toward it, but it’s not always direct. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice is directly influencing your behavior when you're doing things like lining up receipts or tapping the calculator?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t always feel like I’m choosing it in a normal way. I’ll be at work and suddenly the receipts are spread out across the floor, like I’m lining them up for some reason, and then I’m aware of people looking at me.\n\nOr I’ll tap the calculator in sets of nine, and it’s not like that’s part of bookkeeping, obviously. My teacher in high school used to make us show our work on calculators, which is funny because now I’m doing the opposite, like it’s not helping at all—sorry, that’s not really the point.\n\nThe point is, yeah, when I’m doing those things, it feels like something is pushing me toward it. Like standing by the copier with my hands up until someone asks if I’m okay… I don’t really know why I did that. It just felt connected somehow.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice is telling you to do these specific things, like it's giving you instructions?\n\nPatient: Sort of, but not like a list of instructions. It’s more like… when I’m alone at my desk, or sometimes walking home from class, I hear this man’s voice say things like, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign.”\n\nAnd it’s clear enough that I’ll actually turn around and look, like someone is right there, but nobody is. I guess “check line seven” sounds like an instruction, especially since I do bookkeeping stuff, but it’s not always… I don’t know, it’s not like a normal person telling me exactly what to do. It just feels like I’m supposed to figure it out.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It’s just… I’m doing these things, like standing by the copier with my hands held up, and then someone asks if I’m okay and I realize how odd it must look.\n\nDoctor: Do these things, like lining up receipts or hearing the voice, happen all the time or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: They come and go, yeah. Around when I started the evening accounting courses while still doing the bookkeeping job.\n\nAt first I thought it was just being tired, because those classes were after work and the fluorescent lights in those rooms are awful—like they buzz in a way that makes numbers feel sharper, if that makes sense. But over time it’s been happening more in patches. Some days I’m mostly okay, and other days it’s like the receipts or the voice or the patterns are harder to ignore.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice you hear is actually trying to communicate something meaningful to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I know it sounds strange, but sometimes it feels like it’s not just random. Like when it says something and then I look at an invoice total or an account number, or even a due date, I get this feeling that it’s kind of… pointing at something about me.\n\nMy supervisor will show me, like, “No, that’s just a normal entry, that’s just how the account is labeled,” and I can understand that when he says it. But part of me still feels like the numbers are tracking whether I understand the pattern or not. Like I’m supposed to notice it.\n\nIt’s hard to explain without sounding ridiculous. The dates especially can feel like they line up too neatly, and then I start thinking maybe the voice is trying to get me to see that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like the voice you hear is telling you to do these things, like lining up receipts or tapping the calculator?\n\nPatient: Sometimes it feels like that, yeah… not exactly “line up the receipts now,” but I’ll hear a man’s voice when I’m alone at my desk, or walking home from class, and it’ll say things like, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign.”\n\nAnd it’s clear enough that I actually look around, like someone was behind me. Which is embarrassing, because once I did that near the bus stop and this lady thought I was looking at her bag or something—anyway. It’s not always telling me to do the tapping exactly, but when it says something like that, I start checking things or looking for what it means.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice is directly telling you to perform these specific actions, like lining up the receipts or tapping the calculator?\n\nPatient: Not like a command exactly… I don’t think it says, “tap the calculator nine times,” like that. But then I’ll find myself doing it, you know? Like tapping in sets of nine, or lining receipts across the floor at work, and afterward I’m kind of standing there thinking, why did I do that?\n\nAnd the copier thing too—I stood there with my hands up by the copier until someone asked if I was okay. I don’t even know why. It’s like the voice or the feeling points me toward it, but it’s not always direct. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice is directly influencing your behavior when you're doing things like lining up receipts or tapping the calculator?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t always feel like I’m choosing it in a normal way. I’ll be at work and suddenly the receipts are spread out across the floor, like I’m lining them up for some reason, and then I’m aware of people looking at me.\n\nOr I’ll tap the calculator in sets of nine, and it’s not like that’s part of bookkeeping, obviously. My teacher in high school used to make us show our work on calculators, which is funny because now I’m doing the opposite, like it’s not helping at all—sorry, that’s not really the point.\n\nThe point is, yeah, when I’m doing those things, it feels like something is pushing me toward it. Like standing by the copier with my hands up until someone asks if I’m okay… I don’t really know why I did that. It just felt connected somehow.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice is telling you to do these specific things, like it's giving you instructions?\n\nPatient: Sort of, but not like a list of instructions. It’s more like… when I’m alone at my desk, or sometimes walking home from class, I hear this man’s voice say things like, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign.”\n\nAnd it’s clear enough that I’ll actually turn around and look, like someone is right there, but nobody is. I guess “check line seven” sounds like an instruction, especially since I do bookkeeping stuff, but it’s not always… I don’t know, it’s not like a normal person telling me exactly what to do. It just feels like I’m supposed to figure it out.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice is directly causing you to do these things, like lining up receipts or tapping the calculator?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say it’s directly causing me, like pushing a button in my head or something. But when I’m alone at my desk, or sometimes walking home from class, I hear this man’s voice pretty clearly.\n\nIt’ll say stuff like, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign,” and I’ll actually look around because it sounds like someone is there. But there isn’t anybody. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean… maybe not exactly because it says “line them up,” but when I’m alone at my desk I do hear this man’s voice sometimes. Like it’ll say, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign,” and it’s clear enough that I look around like someone is standing there.

I mean, with the invoices and stuff, it can feel like the totals or account numbers aren’t just random. Like they’re… pointing at me somehow, or like the due dates line up in a way that’s meant for me to notice.\n\nI know that sounds weird. My supervisor will show me it’s just a normal entry, like, “See, this is just from this vendor,” and I’ll understand what he’s saying, but I still kind of feel like they’re tracking whether I get the pattern or not. It’s hard to explain.\n\nNumbers have always stuck in my head, though. Like I used to remember everyone’s locker combinations in school, not because I was trying to be creepy, just because numbers kind of stay there. Or I’ll tap the calculator in sets of nine. It’s not every minute or anything. But it’s been… building, I guess, for about ten months now. Like when it says something and then I look at an invoice total or an account number, or even a due date, I get this feeling that it’s kind of… pointing at something about me.\n\nMy supervisor will show me, like, “No, that’s just a normal entry, that’s just how the account is labeled,” and I can understand that when he says it. But part of me still feels like the numbers are tracking whether I understand the pattern or not. Like I’m supposed to notice it.\n\nIt’s hard to explain without sounding ridiculous. Which is embarrassing, because once I did that near the bus stop and this lady thought I was looking at her bag or something—anyway. Yeah… kind of. Not like it says, “tap the calculator” or “line up the receipts,” exactly. It’s more that when I’m by myself at my desk, or sometimes walking home from class, I hear this man’s voice say things like, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign.”

Like when it says something and then I look at an invoice total or an account number, or even a due date, I get this feeling that it’s kind of… pointing at something about me.\n\nMy supervisor will show me, like, “No, that’s just a normal entry, that’s just how the account is labeled,” and I can understand that when he says it. But part of me still feels like the numbers are tracking whether I understand the pattern or not. Like I’m supposed to notice it.\n\nIt’s hard to explain without sounding ridiculous. Which is embarrassing, because once I did that near the bus stop and this lady thought I was looking at her bag or something—anyway. It’s not always telling me to do the tapping exactly, but when it says something like that, I start checking things or looking for what it means.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice is directly telling you to perform these specific actions, like lining up the receipts or tapping the calculator?\n\nPatient: Not like a command exactly… I don’t think it says, “tap the calculator nine times,” like that. But part of me still feels like the numbers are tracking whether I understand the pattern or not. Like I’m supposed to notice it.\n\nIt’s hard to explain without sounding ridiculous. The dates especially can feel like they line up too neatly, and then I start thinking maybe the voice is trying to get me to see that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like the voice you hear is telling you to do these things, like lining up receipts or tapping the calculator?\n\nPatient: Sometimes it feels like that, yeah… not exactly “line up the receipts now,” but I’ll hear a man’s voice when I’m alone at my desk, or walking home from class, and it’ll say things like, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign.”\n\nAnd it’s clear enough that I actually look around, like someone was behind me. Which is embarrassing, because once I did that near the bus stop and this lady thought I was looking at her bag or something—anyway. It’s not always telling me to do the tapping exactly, but when it says something like that, I start checking things or looking for what it means.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice is directly telling you to perform these specific actions, like lining up the receipts or tapping the calculator?\n\nPatient: Not like a command exactly… I don’t think it says, “tap the calculator nine times,” like that. But then I’ll find myself doing it, you know? Like tapping in sets of nine, or lining receipts across the floor at work, and afterward I’m kind of standing there thinking, why did I do that?\n\nAnd the copier thing too—I stood there with my hands up by the copier until someone asked if I was okay. I don’t even know why. It’s like the voice or the feeling points me toward it, but it’s not always direct. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice is directly influencing your behavior when you're doing things like lining up receipts or tapping the calculator?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t always feel like I’m choosing it in a normal way. I’ll be at work and suddenly the receipts are spread out across the floor, like I’m lining them up for some reason, and then I’m aware of people looking at me.\n\nOr I’ll tap the calculator in sets of nine, and it’s not like that’s part of bookkeeping, obviously. My teacher in high school used to make us show our work on calculators, which is funny because now I’m doing the opposite, like it’s not helping at all—sorry, that’s not really the point.\n\nThe point is, yeah, when I’m doing those things, it feels like something is pushing me toward it. I guess “check line seven” sounds like an instruction, especially since I do bookkeeping stuff, but it’s not always… I don’t know, it’s not like a normal person telling me exactly what to do. It just feels like I’m supposed to figure it out.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice is directly causing you to do these things, like lining up receipts or tapping the calculator?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say it’s directly causing me, like pushing a button in my head or something. I don’t think it’s like a command, exactly. It’s not usually saying, “do this right now.” It’s more like… I’ll be alone at my desk, or walking home from class, and I hear this man’s voice say something like, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign.”

Then I get hung up on what it means. I mean, line seven could be anything if you’re doing bookkeeping, you know, like on a form or a receipt… but no, I don’t think it’s a straight-up command I have to follow.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you feel like the voice is directly influencing your actions when you line up receipts or tap the calculator?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 22 / bookkeeping_accounting_or_auditing_clerk / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Hallucinations\nmanifestation: \"Sometimes when I am alone at my desk or walking home from class, I hear a man's voice say things like 'check line seven' or 'you missed the sign,' clear enough that I look around even though nobody is there.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I guess… indirectly, maybe. Like I’ve said, it’s not usually telling me, “put that receipt there” or “tap the calculator.” It’s more that I’ll be alone at my desk, or sometimes walking home from class, and I hear this man’s voice say stuff like, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign.”

And it sounds close, like real enough that I actually look around for somebody. Nobody’s there, obviously, but then my brain sort of grabs onto it. Like with receipts, line seven makes me think of forms and numbers and whether I missed something, so I start messing with things. I don’t know if that’s the voice making me do it or me trying to figure out what it meant.

---------- turn 17 | doctor:inference | doctor.build_inference_prompt() | model=meta-llama/Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct ----------

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF23, F21, F28\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… people at work started noticing I’m doing weird stuff that doesn’t really make sense. Like, I work with receipts and numbers, and I’ll end up lining receipts across the floor instead of filing them. Or I keep tapping the calculator in sets of nine, which sounds stupid when I say it out loud.\n\nAnd the copier thing too—I’ve been standing there with my hands up, just kind of stuck there, until someone asks if I’m okay. The copier at my school used to jam all the time too, actually, and I always thought it had this one specific sound before it messed up, but… yeah, I guess that’s not the point. I’m here because I’m doing things like that and people are getting concerned.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform these actions, like lining receipts or tapping the calculator, or is it more that you just find yourself doing them?\n\nPatient: It’s more like I just find myself doing it, I think. Like I’ll be at work with the receipts and then they’re across the floor in these lines, and I didn’t exactly plan that. Or I’m tapping the calculator in nines and then I realize someone is watching me.\n\nThe copier thing is weird too—I’ll just be standing there with my hands up, like I’m waiting for something, and then someone has to ask if I’m okay. I don’t know if I “have to” do it. It just sort of happens, and then it looks strange.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel distressed or anxious when you realize you're doing these things, like lining up receipts or standing by the copier?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it anxious exactly. Sometimes I feel embarrassed when I notice somebody saw it, like when you realize your shirt’s been inside out all morning or something. That happened to me once in high school, actually, and nobody told me until lunch, which was kind of… yeah.\n\nWith the receipts or the copier, it’s more like I come back to myself and think, “Oh, I’m doing that again.” I don’t always know what I’m feeling right then. Maybe uncomfortable, but I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that there's a special meaning or connection between things that might not be obvious to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I mean, with the invoices and stuff, it can feel like the totals or account numbers aren’t just random. Like they’re… pointing at me somehow, or like the due dates line up in a way that’s meant for me to notice.\n\nI know that sounds weird. My supervisor will show me it’s just a normal entry, like, “See, this is just from this vendor,” and I’ll understand what he’s saying, but I still kind of feel like they’re tracking whether I get the pattern or not. It’s hard to explain.\n\nNumbers have always stuck in my head, though. Like I used to remember everyone’s locker combinations in school, not because I was trying to be creepy, just because numbers kind of stay there. But with the accounts it feels different, like there’s a message in it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear voices or sounds that other people can't hear?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yeah. Not all the time. It’s usually when I’m by myself, like at my desk, or walking home from class, which is funny because I don’t even take classes now, but I still think of it that way because the route goes past the school buildings.\n\nIt’s like a man’s voice, pretty clear. It’ll say something like, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign,” and I’ll actually look around because it sounds like someone said it near me. But then there’s nobody there. It’s not like a whole conversation, usually just a phrase.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you must perform these actions, like lining receipts or tapping the calculator, in order to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like… “if I don’t do this, something terrible happens.” It’s more that I just start doing it and it feels like it has to be finished a certain way.\n\nLike at work I’ll line receipts across the floor, which I know doesn’t make sense because then people have to step around them, and that’s not even where receipts go. Or I’ll tap the calculator in sets of nine. Nine is a weird number because it looks like a six upside down, which used to bother me on worksheets in school if the page got turned around—\n\nBut, yeah, I don’t think I’m preventing something bad. It’s just… I’m doing these things, like standing by the copier with my hands held up, and then someone asks if I’m okay and I realize how odd it must look.\n\nDoctor: Do these things, like lining up receipts or hearing the voice, happen all the time or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: They come and go, yeah. It’s not every minute or anything. But it’s been… building, I guess, for about ten months now. Around when I started the evening accounting courses while still doing the bookkeeping job.\n\nAt first I thought it was just being tired, because those classes were after work and the fluorescent lights in those rooms are awful—like they buzz in a way that makes numbers feel sharper, if that makes sense. But over time it’s been happening more in patches. Some days I’m mostly okay, and other days it’s like the receipts or the voice or the patterns are harder to ignore.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice you hear is actually trying to communicate something meaningful to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I know it sounds strange, but sometimes it feels like it’s not just random. Like when it says something and then I look at an invoice total or an account number, or even a due date, I get this feeling that it’s kind of… pointing at something about me.\n\nMy supervisor will show me, like, “No, that’s just a normal entry, that’s just how the account is labeled,” and I can understand that when he says it. But part of me still feels like the numbers are tracking whether I understand the pattern or not. Like I’m supposed to notice it.\n\nIt’s hard to explain without sounding ridiculous. The dates especially can feel like they line up too neatly, and then I start thinking maybe the voice is trying to get me to see that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like the voice you hear is telling you to do these things, like lining up receipts or tapping the calculator?\n\nPatient: Sometimes it feels like that, yeah… not exactly “line up the receipts now,” but I’ll hear a man’s voice when I’m alone at my desk, or walking home from class, and it’ll say things like, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign.”\n\nAnd it’s clear enough that I actually look around, like someone was behind me. Which is embarrassing, because once I did that near the bus stop and this lady thought I was looking at her bag or something—anyway. It’s not always telling me to do the tapping exactly, but when it says something like that, I start checking things or looking for what it means.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice is directly telling you to perform these specific actions, like lining up the receipts or tapping the calculator?\n\nPatient: Not like a command exactly… I don’t think it says, “tap the calculator nine times,” like that. But then I’ll find myself doing it, you know? Like tapping in sets of nine, or lining receipts across the floor at work, and afterward I’m kind of standing there thinking, why did I do that?\n\nAnd the copier thing too—I stood there with my hands up by the copier until someone asked if I was okay. I don’t even know why. It’s like the voice or the feeling points me toward it, but it’s not always direct. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice is directly influencing your behavior when you're doing things like lining up receipts or tapping the calculator?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t always feel like I’m choosing it in a normal way. I’ll be at work and suddenly the receipts are spread out across the floor, like I’m lining them up for some reason, and then I’m aware of people looking at me.\n\nOr I’ll tap the calculator in sets of nine, and it’s not like that’s part of bookkeeping, obviously. My teacher in high school used to make us show our work on calculators, which is funny because now I’m doing the opposite, like it’s not helping at all—sorry, that’s not really the point.\n\nThe point is, yeah, when I’m doing those things, it feels like something is pushing me toward it. Like standing by the copier with my hands up until someone asks if I’m okay… I don’t really know why I did that. It just felt connected somehow.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice is telling you to do these specific things, like it's giving you instructions?\n\nPatient: Sort of, but not like a list of instructions. It’s more like… when I’m alone at my desk, or sometimes walking home from class, I hear this man’s voice say things like, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign.”\n\nAnd it’s clear enough that I’ll actually turn around and look, like someone is right there, but nobody is. I guess “check line seven” sounds like an instruction, especially since I do bookkeeping stuff, but it’s not always… I don’t know, it’s not like a normal person telling me exactly what to do. It just feels like I’m supposed to figure it out.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice is directly causing you to do these things, like lining up receipts or tapping the calculator?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say it’s directly causing me, like pushing a button in my head or something. But when I’m alone at my desk, or sometimes walking home from class, I hear this man’s voice pretty clearly.\n\nIt’ll say stuff like, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign,” and I’ll actually look around because it sounds like someone is there. But there isn’t anybody. So… I guess it feels connected, but I’m not sure I can say it’s exactly making me do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're lining up receipts or tapping the calculator because the voice is telling you to?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe not exactly because it says “line them up,” but when I’m alone at my desk I do hear this man’s voice sometimes. Like it’ll say, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign,” and it’s clear enough that I look around like someone is standing there.\n\nNobody is, though. And then I get stuck thinking I’m supposed to do something with it. The calculator thing kind of… I don’t know, it feels related, but the voice isn’t always naming the exact thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing these things, like lining up receipts or tapping the calculator, because of what the voice is saying?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. Not like it says, “tap the calculator” or “line up the receipts,” exactly. It’s more that when I’m by myself at my desk, or sometimes walking home from class, I hear this man’s voice say things like, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign.”\n\nIt sounds real enough that I’ll turn and look around, like someone’s behind me, but nobody is there. Then I get caught up trying to figure out what it means, and I guess that’s when I start doing those things. Like I’m trying to make sure I didn’t miss something.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice is directly telling you to perform these actions, like a command you must follow?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s like a command, exactly. It’s not usually saying, “do this right now.” It’s more like… I’ll be alone at my desk, or walking home from class, and I hear this man’s voice say something like, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign.”\n\nAnd it’s clear enough that I look around, like I really think someone said it near me, but nobody’s there. Then I get hung up on what it means. I mean, line seven could be anything if you’re doing bookkeeping, you know, like on a form or a receipt… but no, I don’t think it’s a straight-up command I have to follow.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice is directly influencing your actions when you line up receipts or tap the calculator?\n\nPatient: I guess… indirectly, maybe. Like I’ve said, it’s not usually telling me, “put that receipt there” or “tap the calculator.” It’s more that I’ll be alone at my desk, or sometimes walking home from class, and I hear this man’s voice say stuff like, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign.”\n\nAnd it sounds close, like real enough that I actually look around for somebody. Nobody’s there, obviously, but then my brain sort of grabs onto it. Like with receipts, line seven makes me think of forms and numbers and whether I missed something, so I start messing with things. I don’t know if that’s the voice making me do it or me trying to figure out what it meant.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

So I guess the stuff I do after is me trying to answer it somehow, or make sure I didn’t miss what it was talking about.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you feel like you're lining up receipts or tapping the calculator as a direct result of what the voice is telling you?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 22 / bookkeeping_accounting_or_auditing_clerk / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Hallucinations\nmanifestation: \"Sometimes when I am alone at my desk or walking home from class, I hear a man's voice say things like 'check line seven' or 'you missed the sign,' clear enough that I look around even though nobody is there.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if I’d say it’s a direct result, exactly. Like, the voice isn’t saying, “tap the calculator” or “line up the receipts.” It’s more like… I’ll be alone at my desk, or walking home from class, and I hear this man’s voice say something like, “check line seven” or “you missed the sign.”

And it’s clear enough that I look around, because it really feels like someone nearby said it. But there’s nobody there. Then I start thinking about it, and numbers and lines and receipts all kind of connect in my head, so I end up doing those things. But I don’t know if that means the voice is telling me to.

---------- turn 19 | doctor:inference | doctor.build_inference_prompt() | model=meta-llama/Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct ----------
========== INPUT [doctor] ==========
[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF23, F21, F28\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… people at work started noticing I’m doing weird stuff that doesn’t really make sense. Like, I work with receipts and numbers, and I’ll end up lining receipts across the floor instead of filing them. Or I keep tapping the calculator in sets of nine, which sounds stupid when I say it out loud.\n\nAnd the copier thing too—I’ve been standing there with my hands up, just kind of stuck there, until someone asks if I’m okay. The copier at my school used to jam all the time too, actually, and I always thought it had this one specific sound before it messed up, but… yeah, I guess that’s not the point. I’m here because I’m doing things like that and people are getting concerned.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform these actions, like lining receipts or tapping the calculator, or is it more that you just find yourself doing them?\n\nPatient: It’s more like I just find myself doing it, I think. Like I’ll be at work with the receipts and then they’re across the floor in these lines, and I didn’t exactly plan that. Or I’m tapping the calculator in nines and then I realize someone is watching me.\n\nThe copier thing is weird too—I’ll just be standing there with my hands up, like I’m waiting for something, and then someone has to ask if I’m okay. I don’t know if I “have to” do it. It just sort of happens, and then it looks strange.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel distressed or anxious when you realize you're doing these things, like lining up receipts or standing by the copier?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it anxious exactly. Sometimes I feel embarrassed when I notice somebody saw it, like when you realize your shirt’s been inside out all morning or something. That happened to me once in high school, actually, and nobody told me until lunch, which was kind of… yeah.\n\nWith the receipts or the copier, it’s more like I come back to myself and think, “Oh, I’m doing that again.” I don’t always know what I’m feeling right then. Maybe uncomfortable, but I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that there's a special meaning or connection between things that might not be obvious to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I mean, with the invoices and stuff, it can feel like the totals or account numbers aren’t just random. Like they’re… pointing at me somehow, or like the due dates line up in a way that’s meant for me to notice.\n\nI know that sounds weird. My supervisor will show me it’s just a normal entry, like, “See, this is just from this vendor,” and I’ll understand what he’s saying, but I still kind of feel like they’re tracking whether I get the pattern or not. It’s hard to explain.\n\nNumbers have always stuck in my head, though. Like I used to remember everyone’s locker combinations in school, not because I was trying to be creepy, just because numbers kind of stay there. But with the accounts it feels different, like there’s a message in it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear voices or sounds that other people can't hear?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yeah. Not all the time. It’s usually when I’m by myself, like at my desk, or walking home from class, which is funny because I don’t even take classes now, but I still think of it that way because the route goes past the school buildings.\n\nIt’s like a man’s voice, pretty clear. It’ll say something like, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign,” and I’ll actually look around because it sounds like someone said it near me. But then there’s nobody there. It’s not like a whole conversation, usually just a phrase.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you must perform these actions, like lining receipts or tapping the calculator, in order to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like… “if I don’t do this, something terrible happens.” It’s more that I just start doing it and it feels like it has to be finished a certain way.\n\nLike at work I’ll line receipts across the floor, which I know doesn’t make sense because then people have to step around them, and that’s not even where receipts go. Or I’ll tap the calculator in sets of nine. Nine is a weird number because it looks like a six upside down, which used to bother me on worksheets in school if the page got turned around—\n\nBut, yeah, I don’t think I’m preventing something bad. It’s just… I’m doing these things, like standing by the copier with my hands held up, and then someone asks if I’m okay and I realize how odd it must look.\n\nDoctor: Do these things, like lining up receipts or hearing the voice, happen all the time or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: They come and go, yeah. It’s not every minute or anything. But it’s been… building, I guess, for about ten months now. Around when I started the evening accounting courses while still doing the bookkeeping job.\n\nAt first I thought it was just being tired, because those classes were after work and the fluorescent lights in those rooms are awful—like they buzz in a way that makes numbers feel sharper, if that makes sense. But over time it’s been happening more in patches. Some days I’m mostly okay, and other days it’s like the receipts or the voice or the patterns are harder to ignore.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice you hear is actually trying to communicate something meaningful to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I know it sounds strange, but sometimes it feels like it’s not just random. Like when it says something and then I look at an invoice total or an account number, or even a due date, I get this feeling that it’s kind of… pointing at something about me.\n\nMy supervisor will show me, like, “No, that’s just a normal entry, that’s just how the account is labeled,” and I can understand that when he says it. But part of me still feels like the numbers are tracking whether I understand the pattern or not. Like I’m supposed to notice it.\n\nIt’s hard to explain without sounding ridiculous. The dates especially can feel like they line up too neatly, and then I start thinking maybe the voice is trying to get me to see that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like the voice you hear is telling you to do these things, like lining up receipts or tapping the calculator?\n\nPatient: Sometimes it feels like that, yeah… not exactly “line up the receipts now,” but I’ll hear a man’s voice when I’m alone at my desk, or walking home from class, and it’ll say things like, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign.”\n\nAnd it’s clear enough that I actually look around, like someone was behind me. Which is embarrassing, because once I did that near the bus stop and this lady thought I was looking at her bag or something—anyway. It’s not always telling me to do the tapping exactly, but when it says something like that, I start checking things or looking for what it means.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice is directly telling you to perform these specific actions, like lining up the receipts or tapping the calculator?\n\nPatient: Not like a command exactly… I don’t think it says, “tap the calculator nine times,” like that. But then I’ll find myself doing it, you know? Like tapping in sets of nine, or lining receipts across the floor at work, and afterward I’m kind of standing there thinking, why did I do that?\n\nAnd the copier thing too—I stood there with my hands up by the copier until someone asked if I was okay. I don’t even know why. It’s like the voice or the feeling points me toward it, but it’s not always direct. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice is directly influencing your behavior when you're doing things like lining up receipts or tapping the calculator?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t always feel like I’m choosing it in a normal way. I’ll be at work and suddenly the receipts are spread out across the floor, like I’m lining them up for some reason, and then I’m aware of people looking at me.\n\nOr I’ll tap the calculator in sets of nine, and it’s not like that’s part of bookkeeping, obviously. My teacher in high school used to make us show our work on calculators, which is funny because now I’m doing the opposite, like it’s not helping at all—sorry, that’s not really the point.\n\nThe point is, yeah, when I’m doing those things, it feels like something is pushing me toward it. Like standing by the copier with my hands up until someone asks if I’m okay… I don’t really know why I did that. It just felt connected somehow.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice is telling you to do these specific things, like it's giving you instructions?\n\nPatient: Sort of, but not like a list of instructions. It’s more like… when I’m alone at my desk, or sometimes walking home from class, I hear this man’s voice say things like, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign.”\n\nAnd it’s clear enough that I’ll actually turn around and look, like someone is right there, but nobody is. I guess “check line seven” sounds like an instruction, especially since I do bookkeeping stuff, but it’s not always… I don’t know, it’s not like a normal person telling me exactly what to do. It just feels like I’m supposed to figure it out.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice is directly causing you to do these things, like lining up receipts or tapping the calculator?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say it’s directly causing me, like pushing a button in my head or something. But when I’m alone at my desk, or sometimes walking home from class, I hear this man’s voice pretty clearly.\n\nIt’ll say stuff like, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign,” and I’ll actually look around because it sounds like someone is there. But there isn’t anybody. So… I guess it feels connected, but I’m not sure I can say it’s exactly making me do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're lining up receipts or tapping the calculator because the voice is telling you to?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe not exactly because it says “line them up,” but when I’m alone at my desk I do hear this man’s voice sometimes. Like it’ll say, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign,” and it’s clear enough that I look around like someone is standing there.\n\nNobody is, though. And then I get stuck thinking I’m supposed to do something with it. The calculator thing kind of… I don’t know, it feels related, but the voice isn’t always naming the exact thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing these things, like lining up receipts or tapping the calculator, because of what the voice is saying?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. Not like it says, “tap the calculator” or “line up the receipts,” exactly. It’s more that when I’m by myself at my desk, or sometimes walking home from class, I hear this man’s voice say things like, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign.”\n\nIt sounds real enough that I’ll turn and look around, like someone’s behind me, but nobody is there. Then I get caught up trying to figure out what it means, and I guess that’s when I start doing those things. Like I’m trying to make sure I didn’t miss something.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice is directly telling you to perform these actions, like a command you must follow?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s like a command, exactly. It’s not usually saying, “do this right now.” It’s more like… I’ll be alone at my desk, or walking home from class, and I hear this man’s voice say something like, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign.”\n\nAnd it’s clear enough that I look around, like I really think someone said it near me, but nobody’s there. Then I get hung up on what it means. I mean, line seven could be anything if you’re doing bookkeeping, you know, like on a form or a receipt… but no, I don’t think it’s a straight-up command I have to follow.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice is directly influencing your actions when you line up receipts or tap the calculator?\n\nPatient: I guess… indirectly, maybe. Like I’ve said, it’s not usually telling me, “put that receipt there” or “tap the calculator.” It’s more that I’ll be alone at my desk, or sometimes walking home from class, and I hear this man’s voice say stuff like, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign.”\n\nAnd it sounds close, like real enough that I actually look around for somebody. Nobody’s there, obviously, but then my brain sort of grabs onto it. Like with receipts, line seven makes me think of forms and numbers and whether I missed something, so I start messing with things. I don’t know if that’s the voice making me do it or me trying to figure out what it meant.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice is telling you to do these things as a way to respond to what it's saying?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… maybe as a response, but not like it gives me instructions. It’s more like when I’m alone at my desk, or sometimes walking home from class, I’ll hear this man’s voice say, like, “check line seven” or “you missed the sign,” and it’s so clear I’ll actually look around.\n\nNobody’s there, but then I start thinking about what it meant. Like, with bookkeeping, “line seven” could be a real thing, you know? Forms have all these lines and little boxes, and if one number is off then everything feels off. So I guess the stuff I do after is me trying to answer it somehow, or make sure I didn’t miss what it was talking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're lining up receipts or tapping the calculator as a direct result of what the voice is telling you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say it’s a direct result, exactly. Like, the voice isn’t saying, “tap the calculator” or “line up the receipts.” It’s more like… I’ll be alone at my desk, or walking home from class, and I hear this man’s voice say something like, “check line seven” or “you missed the sign.”\n\nAnd it’s clear enough that I look around, because it really feels like someone nearby said it. But there’s nobody there. Then I start thinking about it, and numbers and lines and receipts all kind of connect in my head, so I end up doing those things. But I don’t know if that means the voice is telling me to.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

Like I’m trying to make sure I didn’t miss something.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice is directly telling you to perform these actions, like a command you must follow?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s like a command, exactly. Then I get hung up on what it means. I mean, line seven could be anything if you’re doing bookkeeping, you know, like on a form or a receipt… but no, I don’t think it’s a straight-up command I have to follow.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice is directly influencing your actions when you line up receipts or tap the calculator?\n\nPatient: I guess… indirectly, maybe. Like I’ve said, it’s not usually telling me, “put that receipt there” or “tap the calculator.” It’s more that I’ll be alone at my desk, or sometimes walking home from class, and I hear this man’s voice say stuff like, “check line seven,” or “you missed the sign.”\n\nAnd it sounds close, like real enough that I actually look around for somebody. Nobody’s there, obviously, but then my brain sort of grabs onto it. Like with receipts, line seven makes me think of forms and numbers and whether I missed something, so I start messing with things. I don’t know if that’s the voice making me do it or me trying to figure out what it meant.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice is telling you to do these things as a way to respond to what it's saying?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… maybe as a response, but not like it gives me instructions.
